=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        currGroup = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(currGroup, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            nextGroup =  kth.next

            # reverse the currGroup
            prev, curr = kth.next, nextGroup
            while curr != nextGroup:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            currGroup.next, currGroup = kth, currGroup.next

            k += 1 

        return dummy.next


    def getKth(self, node, k):
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

from typing import Optional

class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        group_prev = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(group_prev, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            group_next = kth.next

            prev, curr = group_next, group_prev.next
            while curr != group_next:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            tmp = group_prev.next
            group_prev.next = kth
            group_prev = tmp

        return dummy.next

    def getKth(self, node: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node

from typing import Optional

class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        group_prev = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(group_prev, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            group_next = kth.next

            prev, curr = group_next, group_prev.next
            while curr != group_next:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            tmp = group_prev.next
            group_prev.next = kth
            group_prev = tmp

        return dummy.next

    def getKth(self, node: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node

Let's ensure code variable names not conflicting with `currGroup`? We can keep original style to minimize changes. Fixed code:

class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        currGroup = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(currGroup, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            nextGroup = kth.next

            prev, curr = nextGroup, currGroup.next
            while curr != nextGroup:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            currGroup.next, currGroup = kth, currGroup.next

        return dummy.next

    def getKth(self, node, k):
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node

This is minimal. But wait: In tuple assignment `currGroup.next, currGroup = kth, currGroup.next`, after reversal, `currGroup.next` is old first? However, after `currGroup.next = kth`, the old first node is no longer accessible via currGroup.next, but RHS stored it. Good.

class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        curr_group = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(curr_group, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            next_group = kth.next

            prev, curr = next_group, curr_group.next
            while curr != next_group:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            tmp = curr_group.next
            curr_group.next = kth
            curr_group = tmp

        return dummy.next

    def getKth(self, node, k):
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node

This is standard. But note variable `tmp` reused. Fine.

from typing import Optional

class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        group_prev = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(group_prev, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            group_next = kth.next

            prev, curr = group_next, group_prev.next
            while curr != group_next:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            tmp = group_prev.next
            group_prev.next = kth
            group_prev = tmp

        return dummy.next

    def getKth(self, node: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node

class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        currGroup = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(currGroup, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            nextGroup = kth.next

            prev, curr = nextGroup, currGroup.next
            while curr != nextGroup:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            tmp = currGroup.next
            currGroup.next = kth
            currGroup = tmp

        return dummy.next

    def getKth(self, node, k):
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node

Let's maybe produce final code:

from typing import Optional

class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        group_prev = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(group_prev, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            group_next = kth.next

            prev, curr = group_next, group_prev.next
            while curr != group_next:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            tmp = group_prev.next
            group_prev.next = kth
            group_prev = tmp

        return dummy.next

    def getKth(self, node: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node

from typing import Optional

class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        group_prev = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(group_prev, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            group_next = kth.next

            prev, curr = group_next, group_prev.next
            while curr != group_next:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            tmp = group_prev.next
            group_prev.next = kth
            group_prev = tmp

        return dummy.next

    def getKth(self, node: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import Optional

class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        group_prev = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(group_prev, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            group_next = kth.next

            prev, curr = group_next, group_prev.next
            while curr != group_next:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            tmp = group_prev.next
            group_prev.next = kth
            group_prev = tmp

        return dummy.next

    def getKth(self, node: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import Optional

class Solution:
    def reverseKGroup(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        dummy = ListNode(0, head)
        group_prev = dummy

        while True:
            kth = self.getKth(group_prev, k)
            if not kth:
                break
            group_next = kth.next

            prev, curr = group_next, group_prev.next
            while curr != group_next:
                tmp = curr.next
                curr.next = prev
                prev = curr
                curr = tmp

            tmp = group_prev.next
            group_prev.next = kth
            group_prev = tmp

        return dummy.next

    def getKth(self, node: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> Optional[ListNode]:
        while node and k:
            node = node.next
            k -= 1
        return node
